     B8CA 834A     
     B8CC 2164     
4266               	; 	RETURN
4267 B8CE C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
4268 B8D0 0450  20 	b *r0
4269               	;
4270               	; 	' ======================================================================
4271               	; 	' DRAWING THE STORE -- four blits, once per screen crossing
4272               	; 	' ======================================================================
4273               	; 	' This is the whole cost of the flip, and it is paid on a discrete event
4274               	; 	' rather than every frame. RallyX's pan repaints 576 chars every time the
4275               	; 	' camera moves one cell; this repaints 640 about once every two seconds
4276               	; 	' of running.
4277               	; 	' SPRITES OFF BEFORE THE BLIT, NOT AFTER IT. This routine WAITs between
4278               	; 	' bands -- one band a frame, because a burst of VDP writes past a few
4279               	; 	' dozen in one frame is silently dropped -- so painting the new screen
4280               	; 	' takes several frames, and for all of them the OLD screen's actors and
4281               	; 	' obstacles were still sitting on top of it. Following Harry through a
4282               	; 	' seam showed him and every cart from the floor he had just left,
4283               	; 	' standing on a shop that was assembling itself underneath them.
4284               	; 	'
4285               	; 	' They are put back by the normal draw on the same pass, so the crossing
4286               	; 	' reads as a cut rather than as a dissolve with ghosts in it.
4287               	; 	' EACH BAND IS FINISHED BEFORE THE FRAME IT IS DRAWN IN ENDS, and the
4288               	; 	' bands go TOP DOWN. Both halves of that matter.
4289               	; 	'
4290               	; 	' The blit paints the template -- shelves, pillars and all -- and the
4291               	; 	' radios and prizes then take out the fixtures they stand in. Those two
4292               	; 	' used to be four frames apart: all four bands blitted with a WAIT
4293               	; 	' between them, and only afterwards the clearing. So every screen change
4294               	; 	' showed its counters and beams for several frames and then erased them,
4295               	; 	' which reads as the shop flickering rather than as it being drawn.
4296               	; 	'
4297               	; 	' TOP DOWN because a band's beam tops are stamped into the SLAB ROW OF
4298               	; 	' THE BAND ABOVE. Going bottom-up, band 1's blit would paint over the
4299               	; 	' beam tops band 0 had just put there. Going top-down the band above is
4300               	; 	' always already drawn, so everything a band needs -- its own cells and
4301               	; 	' the row it borrows overhead -- is final by the time its WAIT arrives.
4302               	; 	'
4303               	; 	' `3 - dq` rather than `FOR dlv = 3 TO 0 STEP -1`: dlv is an unsigned

4318               	; 		' WAIT FIRST, NOT LAST. A band is blitted and then corrected -- the
4319               	; 		' beam top stamped on, the pillar under a radio taken off -- and the
4320               	; 		' raster does not stop while that happens. Starting the burst in the
4321               	; 		' middle of a frame let the raster pass the band between the blit and
4322               	; 		' the correction, so the uncorrected version was shown for a frame:
4323               	; 		' at round start, after CLS, that reads as a support beam appearing
4324               	; 		' and being rubbed out. Beginning at vblank gives the whole burst a
4325               	; 		' clear run at the rows before the beam reaches them. It also still
4326               	; 		' gives each band its own frame, which is what stops a VDP write
4327               	; 		' burst past a few dozen from being silently dropped.

4416               	;
4417               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4418               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4419               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4420               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4421               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4422               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4423               	; 	'
4424               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4425               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4426               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4427               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4428               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4429               	; 	'
4430               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4431               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4432               	; 	' above it at all.
4433               	; 	' BANDS 0 TO 2 -- ALL THREE SHOPPING FLOORS. It used to stop at 1,
4434               	; 	' because band 2's ceiling is the ROOF DECK and the deck had no green
4435               	; 	' under it: a beam there already ran straight into grey and needed
4436               	; 	' nothing. Giving the roof its three green rows (so the lift shaft stops
4437               	; 	' under it, like every other floor) took that away, and the top floor's
4438               	; 	' beams started falling three pixels short.
4439               	; 	'
4440               	; 	' So the roof gets its own version of the stamp. A beam HOLDS THE ROOF
4441               	; 	' UP and must reach it; the shaft merely stops beneath it.
4442               	; beam_one:
4443               cvb_BEAM_ONE

4977               	; 			' NO SPRITE HAZARD ON THE FLOOR THE CROOK IS STANDING ON,
4978               	; 			' AND THE DECISION IS MADE HERE -- once, as the screen is
4979               	; 			' drawn, and never revisited while it is on screen.
4980               	; 			'
4981               	; 			' It was a test in the draw loop and another in the
4982               	; 			' collision test, both re-evaluated every frame, so the
4983               	; 			' hazards came and went as Harry walked on and off the
4984               	; 			' screen: run him to the edge and a floor's worth of balls
4985               	; 			' appeared out of nothing behind him. A rule about what
4986               	; 			' this screen CONTAINS cannot be a per-frame question --
4987               	; 			' the answer changes while the player is looking at it.
4988               	; 			'
4989               	; 			' Zeroing the kind here also makes both of those tests
4990               	; 			' unnecessary: an obstacle that was never loaded cannot be
4991               	; 			' drawn and cannot hit anybody.
4992               	; 			'
4993               	; 			' Radios are exempt. They are characters rather than
4994               	; 			' sprites, they do not move, and a fixture the crook runs

5157 BF60 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5158               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
5159               	; 			'
5160               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
5161               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
5162               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
5163               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
5164               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
5165               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
5166               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
5167               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
5168               	; 			'
5169               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
5170               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
5171               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
5172               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
5173               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
5174               	; 			'
5175               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
5176               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
5177               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
5178               	; 			'
5179               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
5180               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
5181               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
5182               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
5183               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
5184               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
5185               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31
5186 BF62 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0
     BF64 21CC     
5187 BF66 0201  20 	li r1,7936   ; 31*256
     BF68 1F00

5841               	; 	' ONE CELL OF THE DOORWAY, from its column (ccl 0-3), its band row
5842               	; 	' (crw 0-3) and the door state (cst). Four columns and four rows, so
5843               	; 	' sixteen cells, and the three interesting things about a cell are all
5844               	; 	' edges: column 0 and column 3 carry the JAMB, row 0 carries the LINTEL
5845               	; 	' and row 3 the SILL.
5846               	; 	'
5847               	; 	' THE SILL IS REVEALED AS THE DOORS PART, which falls out of this rather
5848               	; 	' than being arranged: shut, every cell is plain door and there is no
5849               	; 	' sill to see; part-open, only the middle two columns show car and sill;
5850               	; 	' open, all four do. A sill sitting under a shut door would be a ledge
5851               	; 	' with nothing behind it.
5852               	; 	'
5853               	; 	' Nested IFs, never `ccl > 0 AND ccl < 3` -- the 9900 backend miscompiles
5854               	; 	' a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).

6131               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
6132               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
6133               	; 		'
6134               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
6135               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
6136               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
6137               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
6138               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
6139               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
6140               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
6141               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
6142               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
6143               	; 		'
6144               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
6145               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
6146               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
6147               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
6148               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
6149               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1

6452               	; 	' --- running
6453               	; 	' THE END WALLS ARE MOVEMENT LIMITS, NOT A CLAMP APPLIED AFTERWARDS.
6454               	; 	'
6455               	; 	' They used to be the latter, and that is the whole bug: on screen 7 Kelly
6456               	; 	' walked on to x = 254, the crossing routine then noticed there was no
6457               	; 	' screen 8 and set him to 232 -- a 22 px snap BACKWARDS. He reached the
6458               	; 	' wall and was thrown off it. Testing the limit BEFORE the step means he
6459               	; 	' arrives at the wall and stays there, which is what a wall does.
6460               	; 	' IN THE AIR HE IS BALLISTIC. The horizontal direction is fixed at
6461               	; 	' take-off and the stick is ignored until he lands: steering in mid-air
6462               	; 	' is not a jump, it is flight, and it also let him flip which way he was
6463               	; 	' facing halfway through an arc. kjdx is 0 for a standing jump, 1 for a
6464               	; 	' running one to the right, 2 to the left.

6779               	;
6780               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
6781               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
6782               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
6783               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
6784               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
6785               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
6786               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
6787               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
6788               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
6789               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
6790               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
6791               	; 	'
6792               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
6793               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
6794               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
6795               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
6796               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.
6797               	; try_esc:

7355               	;
7356               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7357               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
7358               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
7359               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7360               	; 	'
7361               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7362               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7363               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7364               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7365               	; 	'
7366               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7367               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7368               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7369               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7370               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7371               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7372               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7373               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7374               	; 	'
7375               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7376               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7377               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7378               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7379               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7380               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7381               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7382               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7383               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7384               	; 	'
7385               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7386               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7387               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7388               	; wipe_shelf:
7389               cvb_WIPE_SHELF
7390               	; 	wsn = 8

7463               	; 	' both into the slab row of the band above, but they mean different
7464               	; 	' things. SLABP is the top of a free-standing pillar: a radio or a prize
7465               	; 	' stands in front of the pillar, so leaving its cap up there hangs a grey
7466               	; 	' stub over the fixture and that is what this routine exists to remove.
7467               	; 	' SLABE and ROOFSE are written ONLY at column 0 and column 31 -- read

7470               	; 	' above. Clearing those took the support out from under the storey above
7471               	; 	' at the very edge of the screen, so the second floor visibly rested on
7472               	; 	' nothing.
7473               	; 	'
7474               	; 	' It could only ever show on the ESCALATOR screens, which is exactly how

7476               	; 	' fills the middle of an escalator band, so that screen's radio or prize
7477               	; 	' is pushed right out against the end wall; on every other screen no

8460               cv584
8461               	;
8462               	; 	' THE QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen (2),
8463               	; 	' so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here and
8464               	; 	' is spent as whole pixels. hsp4 = 6 walks 1,2,1,2 (1.5 px/frame); 7
8465               	; 	' walks 1,2,2,2 over four frames (1.75); 8 is a flat 2. Two IFs, not a
8466               	; 	' loop and not a divide: hacc is under 4 on entry and hsp4 is at most 8,
8467               	; 	' so it can never need a third. `%` and `/` both compile to a real
8468               	; 	' TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this runs every frame.
8469               	; 	'
8470               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
8471               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
8472               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
8473               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
8474               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
8475               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
8476               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
8477               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
8478               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
8479               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
8480               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
8481               	; 	' flight looking busy.
8482               	; 	'
8483               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
8484               	; 	'
8485               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
8486               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
8487               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
8488               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
8489               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
8490               	; 	'
8491               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,
8492               	; 	' which expired somewhere in the middle of a floor and turned him round
8493               	; 	' for no reason the player could see. Running until the wall means every
8494               	; 	' change of direction has something on screen behind it.
8495               	; 	IF hrun > 0 THEN
8496 D866 D020  34 	movb @cvb_HRUN,r0
     D868 21A8

9037               	;
9038               	; 	' ======================================================================
9039               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
9040               	; 	' ======================================================================
9041               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
9042               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
9043               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
9044               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
9045               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
9046               	; 	'
9047               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
9048               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
9049               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
9050               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
9051               	; 	' broken.
9052               	; 	'
9053               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
9054               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
9055               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
9056               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
9057               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
9058               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
9059               	; 	'
9060               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
9061               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
9062               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
9063               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
9064               	; coll_obst:
9065               cvb_COLL_OBST
9066               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
9067 DCA2 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     DCA4 2190

     DCA8 0300

     DCB0 DCB6

9601               	; 	' NO +8 ON EITHER SIDE, AND THAT IS THE FIX RATHER THAN A TIDY-UP. Both
9602               	; 	' centres are the left edge plus eight, so the eight cancels in the
9603               	; 	' difference -- but adding it first OVERFLOWED THE BYTE. Harry walks to
9604               	; 	' x 253 before he crosses a seam, and 253 + 8 wraps to 5, so a Kop
9605               	; 	' standing at the far LEFT of the screen measured five pixels to a crook
9606               	; 	' walking off the far RIGHT and arrested him across the whole store.
9607               	; 	'
9608               	; 	' Nothing warned: both are plain 8-bit variables, the wrap is silent, and
9609               	; 	' the catch it produces looks like a generous hitbox rather than like
9610               	; 	' arithmetic. The obstacle test has the same shape but cannot reach it --
9611               	; 	' obx tops out at 240 and klx at XWALL, so neither sum passes 255.

9675               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
9676               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
9677               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
9678               	; 	'
9679               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
9680               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
9681               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
9682               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
9683               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
9684               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
9685               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.

9830               	; 		' FOUR RUN FRAMES FROM TWO BITS of the animation counter, and no
9831               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
9832               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
9833               	; 		'
9834               	; 		' KELLY USES BITS 2 AND 3 AND HARRY USES 3 AND 4, because a stride
9835               	; 		' has to cover about a stride's worth of ground or the figure
9836               	; 		' skates. Kelly runs 4 px a frame, so four frames a pose is 16 px;
9837               	; 		' Harry runs 1.75, so eight frames a pose is 14. Matching the two
9838               	; 		' rates to the two speeds is what keeps both looking like running.
9839               	; 		kq = P_KLEG1
9840 E2F4 0200  20 	li r0,14336   ; 56*256

10102               	; 	' same phase arithmetic is two clocks, and the day one of them changed
10103               	; 	' the shoes would walk half a stride behind the feet.

     E554 21A1     
10111               	; 	' HIS ARMS SWING, on the same bit of the animation counter that picks the
10112               	; 	' legs -- so the arm that is forward is the one opposite the leading leg,
10113               	; 	' which is what running looks like.
10114               	; 	' THE ARMS SWING ONCE PER FULL LEG CYCLE, ON BIT 16 -- not on bit 8.
10115               	; 	'
10116               	; 	' Bit 8 is one BEAT, and the legs take four of them (bits 8 and 16). So an
10117               	; 	' arm swing keyed to bit 8 completed twice for every one pass of the legs:
10118               	; 	' the top half of him flapping at double the speed of the bottom half.
10119               	; 	' That is the jig. It is not a drawing problem and no amount of redrawing
10120               	; 	' the legs would have touched it.
10121               	; 	'
10122               	; 	' On bit 16 the arms hold through beats 1-2 and swap for 3-4, which is
10123               	; 	' also the right PHASE: beat 1 leads with one leg and beat 3 with the
10124               	; 	' other, so the arm opposite the leading leg is forward in each -- which
10125               	; 	' is what running looks like.
10126               	; 	hp = P_HBODY
10127 E556 0200  20 	li r0,18432   ; 72*256

10184               	; 	' HE IS STRIPED FROM CAP TO HEM. Both stripe colours run the whole upper
10185               	; 	' half, so both boxes span rows 0-15 and neither can be tucked away --
10186               	; 	' affordable only because Kelly's hat moved into a box of its own. His
10187               	; 	' FACE is pushed DOWN instead (hy+3, box 3..18) so it clears the cap
10188               	; 	' rows, where his two stripe boxes plus Kelly's hat and face already
10189               	; 	' make four. Worst line of a meeting: exactly four, nothing dropped.
10190               	; 	' HE RISES A PIXEL ON THE RECOVERY BEATS. A runner is highest at mid-flight
10191               	; 	' and lowest at contact; with the body pinned at a constant y, alternating
10192               	; 	' legs under a motionless torso reads as a dance rather than as travel.
10193               	; 	'
10194               	; 	' One pixel is enough at this size, and it lifts the BODY ONLY -- the legs
10195               	; 	' keep their y, so the planted foot stays on the floor and the figure
10196               	; 	' stretches at the waist instead of hopping. Lifting the legs too would be
10197               	; 	' a jump.
10198               	; 	'
10199               	; 	' Beats 2 and 4 are the recoveries, which is exactly `hanim AND 8`.
10200               	; 	'
10201               	; 	' Safe because HARRY'S COLLISION HAS NO VERTICAL COMPONENT: he is caught on
10202               	; 	' `same level AND |dx| < CATCHR`. Doing this to Kelly would desynchronise
10203               	; 	' his sprite from the ball and biplane windows in DESIGN.md 5a, which is
10204               	; 	' why it is not done to him.
10205               	; 	' THE WHOLE FIGURE RISES, legs included, rather than the body stretching
10206               	; 	' away from the feet. Two reasons, and the second was not the first plan:
10207               	; 	'
10208               	; 	'   * It is what a run does. Mid-flight BOTH feet are off the ground, so
10209               	; 	'     lifting the legs with the body is the correct pose, not a compromise.
10210               	; 	'   * Lifting the body alone opens a one-pixel gap at the waist, and the
10211               	; 	'     torso's bottom row and the legs' top row do not align across it --
10212               	; 	'     green shows through and he reads as coming apart.
10213               	; 	'
10214               	; 	' It also keeps `hfy = hy - 7` and `hy2 = hy + 16` LITERAL, which is not a
10215               	; 	' cosmetic concern: assets/checkbands.py reads each band's draw offset by
10216               	; 	' parsing those exact expressions. Bobbing a differently-named variable
10217               	; 	' made them unreadable and the gate failed the build -- correctly. A
10218               	; 	' checker that can no longer see what it checks has to fail, not shrug.

10686 E9B2 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10687 E9B4 0450  20 	b *r0
10688               	;
10689               	; 	' ======================================================================
10690               	; 	' THE SCANNER
10691               	; 	' ======================================================================
10692               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
10693               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
10694               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
10695               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
10696               	; 	'
10697               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
10698               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
10699               	; 	'
10700               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
10701               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
10702               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
10703               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
10704               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
10705               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
10706               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
10707               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
10708               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
10709               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
10710               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
10711               	; esc_tick:
10712               cvb_ESC_TICK
10713               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
10714               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
10715               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
10716               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
10717               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
10718 E9B6 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E9B8 217E     
10719 E9BA 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     E9BC 0100     
10720 E9BE 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10721 E9C0 1402  14 	jhe cv814
10722 E9C2 0460  28 	b @cv813
     E9C4 E9DA     
10723               cv814
10724               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
10725 E9C6 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E9C8 217E     
10726 E9CA 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     E9CC 0700     
10727 E9CE 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10728 E9D0 1A02  14 	jl cv816
10729 E9D2 0460  28 	b @cv815
     E9D4 E9DA     
10730               cv816
10731 E9D6 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10732 E9D8 0450  20 	b *r0
10733               cv815
10734               	; 	END IF
10735               cv813
10736               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
10737               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
10738               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
10739               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
10740               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
10741               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
10742               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
10743               	; 	'
10744               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
10745               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
10746               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
10747               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
10748               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
10749               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
10750               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
10751               	; 	escp = escp + 1
10752 E9DA D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     E9DC 20B0     
10753 E9DE 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     E9E0 0100     
10754 E9E2 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     E9E4 20B0     
10755               	; 	IF escp > 3 THEN escp = 0
10756 E9E6 0201  20 	li r1,1024   ; 4*256
     E9E8 0400     
10757 E9EA 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10758 E9EC 1402  14 	jhe cv818
10759 E9EE 0460  28 	b @cv817
     E9F0 E9F8     
10760               cv818
10761 E9F2 04C0  14 	clr r0
10762 E9F4 D800  38 	movb r0,@cvb_ESCP
     E9F6 20B0     
10763               cv817
10764               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
10765               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
10766               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
10767               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
10768               	; 	' 7 an east one.
10769               	; 	'
10770               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
10771               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
10772               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
10773               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
10774               	; 	'
10775               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
10776               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
10777               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
10778               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
10779               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
10780               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
10781               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
10782               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.
10783               	; 	'
10784               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never
10785               	; 	' move at all.
10786               	; 	IF klsc = 0 THEN

11045 EC34 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11046 EC36 0450  20 	b *r0
11047               	;
11048               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
11049               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
11050               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
11051               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
11052               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
11053               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
11054               	; 	'
11055               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
11056               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
11057               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
11058               	; 	'
11059               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
11060               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
11061               	; 	'   +2  GREY
11062               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
11063               	; 	'
11064               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
11065               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
11066               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
11067               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
11068               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
11069               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
11070               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
11071               	; 	'
11072               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
11073               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
11074               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
11075               	; 	'
11076               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
11077               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
11078               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
11079               	; scan_furn:
11080               cvb_SCAN_FURN

11498               	;
11499               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
11500               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
11501               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
11502               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
11503               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
11504               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11505               	; scan_wipe:
11506               cvb_SCAN_WIPE
11507               	; 	' THIS ADDRESS IS A CHARACTER CODE IN DISGUISE, AND IT WENT STALE.
11508               	; 	' 4096 is the pattern table's BOTTOM THIRD -- rows 16-23, which is
11509               	; 	' band 0 and the radar together -- and the canvas characters begin at
11510               	; 	' genart's SCAN_FIRST, so the base is 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8. It was
11511               	; 	' written out by hand as 4096 + 160*8 when SCAN_FIRST was 160; art
11512               	; 	' added later moved SCAN_FIRST to 208 and nothing moved this with it,
11513               	; 	' so the wipe zeroed the 48 characters BELOW the canvas and never
11514               	; 	' touched the canvas at all.
11515               	; 	'
11516               	; 	' Two of those forty-eight are STRUCTURE. ENDWALL is the building's
11517               	; 	' outside wall and SLABP is a pillar's cap. Blanking a pattern in ONE
11518               	; 	' THIRD leaves a character that draws correctly everywhere else and
11519               	; 	' falls back to its own background colour here -- so the GROUND FLOOR's
11520               	; 	' outside wall turned into plain green while the identical wall one
11521               	; 	' storey up was perfect, and the ground floor's pillars lost the brick
11522               	; 	' carried up through the bar above them. ENDWALL appears only on
11523               	; 	' screens 0 and 7, which is exactly why it was reported as happening
11524               	; 	' only on the escalator screens, mirrored side to side.
11525               	; 	'
11526               	; 	' The name table was correct the whole time. Probing it said so, four
11527               	; 	' times. It was the GLYPH that was empty, in one third only -- which a
11528               	; 	' probe that copies the character somewhere else to look at it cannot
11529               	; 	' see, because the copy renders from a different third's pattern.
11530               	; 	'
11531               	; 	' renumber.py rewrites this line from genart.SCAN_FIRST, and
11532               	; 	' checkstruct.py fails the build if the two disagree.
11533               	; 	#swa = 5760			' 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8, the canvas patterns
11534 EF8E 0200  20 	li r0,5760
     EF90 1680     
11535 EF92 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__SWA
     EF94 20D8

     F020 21D5     
11604               	;
11605               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
11606               	; 	'
11607               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
11608               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
11609               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
11610               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
11611               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
11612               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
11613               	; 	'
11614               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
11615               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
11616               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
11617               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
11618               	; 	'
11619               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
11620               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
11621               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
11622               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
11623               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
11624               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
11625               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
11626               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
11627               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
11628               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
11629               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.

12336               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
12337               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
12338               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
12339               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
12340               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
12341               	; 	'
12342               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
12343               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
12344               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
12345               	; 	'
12346               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
12347               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
12348               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
12349               	; 	'
12350               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
12351               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12352               	; bonus_count:
12353               cvb_BONUS_COUNT
12354               	; 	#bval = 100

     F6DA F62C     
12455               	;
12456               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
12457               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
12458               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
12459               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
12460               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
12461               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
12462               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
12463               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
12464               	; 	'
12465               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
12466               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
12467               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12468               	;
12469               	; do_escape:
12470               cvb_DO_ESCAPE

     F80A AA16     
12603               	;
12604               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
12605               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
12606               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
12607               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
12608               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
12609               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
12610               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
12611               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
12612               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
12613               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
12614               	; 	' Harry was caught.

12713               	;
12714               	; 	' ======================================================================
12715               	; 	' SOUND
12716               	; 	' ======================================================================
12717               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
12718               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
12719               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
12720               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
12721               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.
12722               	; sfx_tick:
12723               cvb_SFX_TICK

13068 FBDE C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
13069 FBE0 0450  20 	b *r0
13070               	;
13071               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
13072               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it.
13073               	; 	'
13074               	; 	' THE FONT IS NOW IN THE BANK TOO, and it used to be deliberately outside:
13075               	; 	' keeping one readable thing in bank 0 means a bank-selection mistake shows
13076               	; 	' as "text survives, art does not" rather than a uniformly blank screen,
13077               	; 	' which is a genuinely useful thing to have when a bank goes wrong.
13078               	; 	'
13079               	; 	' It was given up for 472 bytes, because the fixed area had 342 left and
13080               	; 	' that diagnostic is worth less than the ability to keep building. Two
13081               	; 	' things make it a fair trade rather than a straight loss:
13082               	; 	'
13083               	; 	'   * `BANK SELECT 1` already runs BEFORE `DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits`
13084               	; 	'     (see setup), so nothing about the read order had to change. If that
13085               	; 	'     ever stops being true the font goes blank at boot, immediately and
13086               	; 	'     unmistakably -- a loud failure, not a subtle one.
13087               	; 	'   * The alternative on the table was banking the 29 PRINT AT literals,
13088               	; 	'     which recovers LESS (about 360 bytes net after the reader routine)
13089               	; 	'     and would blind assets/checklayout.py, whose whole method is parsing
13090               	; 	'     `PRINT AT n,"literal"` to catch a string running past column 31 or
13091               	; 	'     a HUD poke landing inside a label. Trading a diagnostic for bytes is
13092               	; 	'     one thing; trading a build gate for fewer bytes is another.
13093               	; 	#if TI994A
13094               	; 	BANK 1
13095      041C     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$
     **** ****     > .rept

13233               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
13234               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
13235               	; 	'
13236               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
13237               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
13238               	; 	' being small sprites.
13239               	; 	'
13240               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
13241               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
13242               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
13243               	; 	'
13244               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
13245               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
13246               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
13247               	;
13248               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
13249               cvb_SPR_KELLY
13250               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00

14125               	;
14126               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
14127               	; 	' Chars 208-255: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
14128               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
14129               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
14130               	;
14131               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
14132               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
14133               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
14134               	; 	' unreadable.
14135               	;
14136               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set

14250               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
14251               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
14252               	; 	'
14253               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
14254               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
14255               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
14256               	; 	'
14257               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
14258               	; 	' blit is
14259               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
14260               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
14261               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
14262               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
14263               	; 	'
14264               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
14265               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
14266               	; 	'
14267               	; 	' template ids:
14268               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
14269               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
14270               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
14271               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
14272               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
14273               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF
14274               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF_W
14275               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF_E
14276               	; 	'   8 T_END_W
14277               	; 	'   9 T_END_E
14278               	;
14279               	; stor_tpl:	' 10 templates x 160 bytes
14280               cvb_STOR_TPL
